### Page 2

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
 歐洲企業協會(Business Europe)發布一項調查顯示，自 6月以來，歐洲企業對工
業和服務業前景的信心有所改善，惟對通膨上升和主權債務問題的擔憂日益加
劇。由於美伊戰爭推高能源成本，而歐盟絕大部分能源依賴進口，能源價格上漲
進一步傳導至零售價格，導致今年通膨升至歐洲央行 2%目標水準之上，市場預
期歐洲央行將於 12月再度升息。
 美國舊金山聯準銀行總裁戴利(Mary Daly)表示，價格壓力正從能源和商品擴散
至服務業，而服務業通膨可能非常頑固。潛在通膨可能並未像市場預期的降
溫。近期能源價格回落，拉低整體通膨，然而核心通膨仍持續往上，聯準會需
要進一步升息以應對居高不下的通膨。此外，聯準會理事克里斯沃勒
(Christopher Waller)及達拉斯聯準銀行總裁洛根(Lorie Logan)均提出升息兩碼的
可能性，多位聯準會官員頻頻釋放偏鷹言論，升息預期升溫，預計將支撐美元

走勢。
 日本 10 月企業商品物價指數年增率由前月的 6.8%上升至 7.1%，主因為中東衝
突及 AI相關需求推升能源和晶片價格，通膨持續上行，提供日本升息有利條件，
有利日幣走升。
 美國 10月 CPI月增率 0.4%、核心 CPI月增率 0.3%雙雙高於預期，10月 PPI年
增率亦升至 6.5%，為四年來新高，通膨居高不下，FedWatch 顯示 12 月升息機
率達九成，2027年 1 月再升息一碼機率逾六成。
 台灣 10月出口 967.6 億美元，為史上單月首度突破 900億美元，年增率 58.9%
為連續 36個月正成長，創下史上最長成長周期。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
    上週台幣匯價高點為 32.794，低點在 32.994，以 32.838 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 7 分。 
    上週台幣走貶，週初，台股反彈，台幣一度走升至 32.794週內高點，不過，外
資延續匯出走勢，台幣隨即收斂所有漲幅，並貶回 32.950 上方；週間，美國公布 10
月 CPI數據表現優於預期，數據強化 12月升息一碼的預期，同時也開始反應升息兩
碼的風險，美元指數強勢突破 103 整數關卡，美元利差優勢擴大，台幣承壓走低至
32.994週內低點；臨近週末，台股走揚，外資轉為買超台股並匯入，台幣升破 32.830，
隨後匯價逢低吸引進口商買盤進場，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.838。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國 10月 CPI優於市場預期，顯
示通膨壓力仍在，市場預期聯準會 12月升息一碼，也不排除一口氣升息兩碼，美元
利差優勢將不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來看，上週公佈台灣 10月出口 967.6億美元，
是史上單月首度突破 900 億美元，寫下單月新高，且年增率 58.9%創下連續 36紅的
表現。基本面強勁，將有助於台幣的表現；盤勢上來看，輝達股價下跌風波，連帶
影響台股表現，整體外資仍偏向匯出，台幣仍面臨貶值的壓力。綜合以上，預估本
週台幣走勢偏弱。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.600-33.200

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。週初，川普表示若伊朗願意簽署具有實質內容的協議，
美方就不太可能重新展開大規模的攻擊，帶動市場進一步降低戰事升溫的預期，油
價下跌，風險偏好回升，減弱美元避險需求，人民幣受益走升至 6.6650；週間，聯
準會官員沃勒鷹派發言，加以美國 10月 CPI表現優於預期，帶動美債殖利率及美元
指數走揚人民幣走貶至 6.6800；臨近週末，美國 10 月 PPI 年增率 6.5%，創下 2022
年 11 月以來高點，顯示通膨壓力難以消退，美元指數高檔震盪，人民幣於 6.6700上
方走勢偏弱，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6746，週線貶值 10點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6726，
週線升值 37點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間震盪。就國際市場來看，美國 10月 CPI優於市場預期，
強化市場對於聯準會 12月升息的預期，利差因素支撐美元指數反彈，將抑制人民幣
走升；就中國國內市場來看，中國最新公布 10 月貿易順差規模持續超過 1000 億美
元，出口商結匯需求將緩解人民幣貶值壓力；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH日線圖
中，USD/CNH重返五日均線之上，顯示人民幣強勢升值的趨勢暫歇，綜合以上，預
估本週人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6600-6.6900

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走弱。週初，USD/JPY 在 158 整數關卡附近波動；週間，美國 10 月
CPI數據表現優於預期，且多位聯準會官員發表鷹派言論，帶動美元指數回升至 102
以上，USD/JPY 上行突破 160 整數關卡；臨近週末，美國 10 月 PPI 年增率 6.5%為
四年來新高，USD/JPY持穩於高位，終場收在 159.66，週線上漲 121 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本公布 10 月企業商品物
價指數年增率由前月的 6.8%上升至 7.1%，主因為中東衝突及 AI相關需求推升能源
和晶片價格，通膨持續上行，提供日本升息有利條件，預計將支撐日幣走勢。二、
美國 10月 CPI月增率 0.4%、核心 CPI月增率 0.3%皆高於預期，10 月 PPI 亦升至高
位，加之多位聯準會官員鷹派發言，FEDWATCH顯示 12月升息機率已上升至 90%，
明年 1 月再升息一碼機率也超過 60%，美元持穩於 102以上高位，將有利 USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月表示近期匯市走勢出現單邊快速波動，日本政府
將繼續與美國財政部密切合作，並採取適當應對措施，日本政府逢高採取干預將限
縮 USD/JPY上行空間。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY突破七周來的主
要交易區間以及年線壓力，在 160 整數關口徘徊，短線多方動能較佔優勢，然而匯
價來至高點有日本政府干預風險，預期將限縮上行幅度。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY
本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：158.50-161.00 
前週交易區間 
157.65 160.18

### Page 6

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/16/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪走跌。週初，市場對於美伊衝突的擔憂有所緩解，風險偏
好回升，國際油價及美元指數同步下行，EUR/USD 自上周低點 1.1135 附近走升最
高來至 1.1250；週間，美國公布 10月份 CPI顯示通膨仍處高位且多位聯準會官員鷹
派談話，EUR/USD 下行跌破 1.11 整數關卡；臨近週末，美國 10 月份 PPI 年增率
6.5%為四年來新高，EUR/USD續跌，終場收在 1.1021，週線下跌 122 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、德國 10 月 CPI 月減 0.2%
年增 2.8%，與預期相符且較 9 月下滑，顯示通膨有所放緩；此外德國央行總裁暨歐
洲央行管委納格爾表示德國經濟可能在第四季加速，儘管能源價格在美國期中選舉
後下跌，但因通膨仍高於 2%的目標，下個月升息依然是顯而易見，歐洲央行維持鷹
派態度將帶給歐元支撐。二、美國 10月 CPI及 PPI 顯示通膨依然頑固且有向更廣泛
經濟領域擴散的趨勢，此外多位聯準會官員發表談話，其中理事沃勒及達拉斯聯儲
總裁洛根均提出升息 2 碼的可能性，升息預期增加將持續支撐美元。三、技術面而
言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 各天期均線向下發散，近期匯價接連跌破 1.12 及 1.11
整數關卡，短線空方動能強勁，短線上以震盪偏弱看待。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD
本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.0900-1.1150 
前週交易區間 
1.1019 1.1251

7  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
                           新台幣利率走勢分析

115.11.09-115.11.13 
銀行隔夜拆款利率 0.805% ~ 0.825%  
30天期短票利率   1.67% ~ 1.68% 
公債－10 年期-A15109/A15109R 1.86%~1.93% 
－ 5年期- A15108 1.71%

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，本週初央行積極減發定存單，令市場資金充裕，雖台股連
日回升，不過資金需求未明顯增加，銀行間交投較為清淡，帶動短率靠向區間
低位。 
週五央行標售 2 年期定存單 250 億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.542%，低於市
場預期區間 1.56%-1.57%，較上次標售結果下跌 3.8 個基本點。 
週五隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.820%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.67%~1.68%，利率高位較上週下跌 1個基本點。 
二、 本週台債殖利率先下後上，週初先落後反應上週美債利率下跌走勢，5 年券
A15108、10年券 A15109分別向下成交在 1.71%、1.86%，後因美債利率反彈，
10 年券 A15109R週五成交在 1.93%。 
受美國 CPI 高於預期、多位官員表達偏鷹派言論、川普表示無須與伊朗達成
協議影響，美債利率回升，加上台灣央行年底升息可能性尚未排除，買盤缺乏
追價動力，預期短線台債利率呈區間盤整，預估 5 年券區間為 1.80%~1.85%，
10 年券區間為 1.90%~2.00%。

廣達(2382 TT) Trading Buy

AI 伺服器展望維持不變，投資建議 Trading Buy 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，
且考慮到 CSP 對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料
能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin 將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，2027
年營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於資料中心建置
的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy 的
投資建議，目標價 324 元。 
3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，稅後 EPS 6.92 元：3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，
QoQ+7.6%；毛利率 4.75%， QoQ-0.27ppts；營業利益 322.04 億
元，QoQ-6.0%；歸屬母公司淨利 284.22 億元，QoQ-0.8%；稅後
EPS 6.92 元。 
PC 市場需求疲弱，伺服器業務支撐營收季增： 3Q26 筆電出貨
960 萬台，QoQ-17%，考慮到筆電零組件漲價帶動 ASP 提高，
我們預估 PC 營收季減高個位數。通用型伺服器營收優於預期，
除 2H26 AI Agent 強勁的需求外，ASP 亦由於零組件漲價而提
升。AI 伺服器部分出貨量較 2Q26 微幅下滑，但同樣受零組件
漲價帶動 ASP 提升影響，預估 AI 伺服器營收仍季增低個位數。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：近期市場對於限制資料中心相關法案
的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單，就現
階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI
伺服器展望將維持不變。我們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin 將可如先
前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收高雙位數增長。考慮
到 AI Agent 的需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器全年
營收呈雙位數年增。 
預估 2027 年營收 5.99 兆元，稅後 EPS 30.85 元：群益預估 2027
年合併營收 5.99 兆元，YoY+44.1%；毛利率 4.18%，YoY-0.6ppts；
營業利益 1,490.08 億元，YoY+21.5%，營益率 2.49%， YoY-
0.5ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 1,266.99 億元，YoY+18.3%，稅後 EPS 
30.85 元。

2 群益投顧 
  ESG 
廣達積極推動環境、社會及公司治理（ESG）永續發展，並將節能減碳、人
才培育及供應鏈管理納入企業營運策略。環境方面，公司於 2024 年通過科
學基礎減碳目標倡議（SBTi）審查，承諾以 2022 年為基準，2030 年前將範
疇一及範疇二溫室氣體排放量降低 42%、範疇三降低 25%，並以 2050 年達
成淨零排放為長期目標。公司亦持續推動再生能源使用及能源管理，2024 年
綠電使用占總用電量 47.51%，並設定 2030 年再生能源使用比例達 50%的目
標。 
社會責任方面，廣達重視員工權益、職業安全及人才培育，遵循責任商業聯
盟（ RBA）行為準則，並透過廣達文教基金會及廣藝基金會推動教育與藝文
活動。公司治理方面，廣達持續強化風險管理、資訊安全及供應鏈永續管理，
並參照 TCFD 及 SASB 等國際框架揭露永續資訊，以提升營運透明度及長期
經營韌性。 
公司簡介 
廣達電腦（Quanta Computer Inc.， 2382 TT）成立於 1988 年，總部位於台灣
桃園，為全球主要電子產品原廠設計製造（ODM）廠商之一，主要業務涵蓋
筆記型電腦、伺服器、雲端運算設備及其他智慧電子產品。公司早期以筆記
型電腦代工為主要業務，憑藉產品研發、供應鏈管理及全球製造能力，建立
與國際品牌客戶的長期合作關係，近年則積極擴展雲端運算及人工智慧相
關業務。 
廣達透過旗下雲達科技（QCT）布局資料中心市場，提供伺服器、儲存設備、
網路交換器及整機櫃解決方案，並隨大型雲端服務供應商（CSP）擴 大 AI 基
礎建設投資，逐步深化 AI 伺服器業務布局。相較傳統筆電代工，AI 伺服器
對系統整合、散熱、電源管理及高速互連技術要求較高，帶動產品複雜度及
單機價值提升，成為公司營收成長的重要驅動因素。 
展望未來，廣達持續推進 AI 伺服器及機櫃級系統整合能力，並透過全球生
產據點布局，強化供應鏈韌性及在地化交付能力。隨 AI 運算需求持續擴張，
伺服器業務可望維持公司中長期成長動能，惟仍須關注 CSP 資本支出、產
品轉換時程及新平台量產效率對營運的影響。 
3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.08 元：3Q26 營收 1.12 兆元，QoQ+7.6%，
YoY+125.2%；毛利率 4.75%，QoQ-0.27ppts，YoY-2.10ppts；營業利益 322.04
億元，QoQ-6.0%，YoY+75.3%，營益率 2.89%，QoQ-0.41ppts，YoY-0.82ppts；
歸屬母公司淨利 284.22 億元，QoQ-0.8%， YoY+73.0%；稅後 EPS 7.08 元。
PC 市場需求疲弱，伺服器業務支撐營收季增： 3Q26 筆電出貨 960 萬台，
QoQ-17%， YoY-24%，出貨量略優於 2Q26 預期的 925 萬台，考慮到筆電零
組件漲價帶動 ASP 提高，我們預估 PC 營收季減高個位數。通用型伺服器營
收優於預期，主要原因除 2H26 AI Agent 強勁的需求外，亦由於 ASP 受零
組件漲價影響而提升。AI 伺服器部分出貨量較 2Q26 微幅下滑，但同樣受零
組件漲價帶動 ASP 提升影響，預估 AI 伺服器營收仍季增低個位數。 
零組件漲價及產品組合改變導致毛利率承壓：由於(1)筆電持續因成本上揚
而壓縮獲利，且出貨數下滑不利經濟規模(2)雖 AI 伺服器出貨量季減，但由

### Page 3

個股報告 
                                        2026 年 11 月 12 日

3 群益投顧 
ASP 提升驅動營收增長，造成毛利率稀釋(3)通用型伺服器出貨量小幅季增，
且 ASP 提升。綜上原因導致 3Q26 毛利率季減 0.27ppts，帶動營益率季減
0.41ppts，此一情景預估將延續至 2027 年，且我們預估 Vera Rubin 世代平
台 ASP 較 GB300 系列接近倍增，雖營收與獲利將可持續上升，但未來 margin
將持續承壓。雖目前有數個專案採用 Consignment model，然而主要以通用
型伺服器與 ASIC 平台為主，且出貨形式由 L6 至 L11 皆有，營收貢獻相對
於採用 B/S 的整櫃 GPU 伺服器而言佔比極低。據我們了解目前已有確定一
個 Vera Rubin 的大型專案及數個規模較小的專案將採用 Consignment 
model，但預估占整體伺服器營收比重仍低，對於毛利率的改善效果有限。
由於 B/S 將稀釋毛利率，Consignment 則會使營收下滑，因此未來針對 ODM
的獲利能力的評估重點應轉向以營業利益年複合成長率(CAGR)或是 ROIC
為主。 
終止華亞科技園區收購，2028 年產能擴充計畫維持不變：廣達表示，由於
對廠方規格及交付時程等事項無法取得共識，因此撤銷友達華亞科技園區
廠房的收購案，原先規劃的產能將透過海外投資計畫或其他彈性應變方案，
維持 2028 年投產的計畫不變。廣達 3Q26 資本支出 128 億元，2026 前三季
累計資本支出達 346 億元，預估 2027 年資本支出介於 400-450 億元，投資
金額成長幅度收斂，將主要用於改良租賃廠房及電力升級。管理層表示 AI
擴廠計畫為配合 CSP 客戶未來 12-24 個月的需求規劃，且伺服器屬於客製
化接單生產(Build-to-Order)，非承擔庫存風險的標準品倍或生產，資本支出
主要投入的項目為廠房電力設施及高階冷卻系統等通用的基礎設施，因此
縱然單一客戶或是特定世代的 AI 伺服器平台需求放緩，亦可無縫轉換產能，
較不會出現過去供過於求時，閒置產能導致的折舊與維護費用等沉沒成本，
營運風險相對較小。 
2027 年伺服器展望不變：美國期中選舉後，市場對於限制資料中心相關法
案的擔憂升溫，然而廣達表示目前客戶方面並未調整訂單。廣達的客戶以五
大 CSP 為主，Neocloud 的占比不高，就現階段來看主要 CSP 的資本支出尚
未放緩，我們預估 2027 年 AI 伺服器展望將維持不變。3Q26 AI 伺服器出貨
量的下滑主要原因為新舊平台交替的陣痛期，預估 4Q26 伺服器出貨量仍將
下滑，但因 Vera Rubin 相較 GB300 近乎倍增的 ASP，AI 伺服器營收將維持
季增。此外，目前在記憶體、PCB 及 CPU 缺貨問題對於消費性產品及通用
型伺服器影響較大，但由於廣達至 2027 年的訂單人以五大 CSP 為主，且
CSP 巨頭鎖料能力較強，因此 AI 伺服器的長短料問題相較之下較輕微，我
們預期 1Q27 Vera Rubin 將可如先前預期放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收
高雙位數增長。通用型伺服器部分，受惠 AI Agent 的應用落地帶動通用型
伺服器需求增長，預估 4Q26 通用型伺服器將持續季增。考慮到 AI Agent 的
需求持續成長，預期 2027 年通用型伺服器除 Share gain 外，亦受惠整體市
場需求提升帶來的成長，全年營收呈雙位數年增。 
管理層預期 2027 年筆電市場持續低迷：管理層預期 2027 年筆電需求將持
續受料件漲價及缺料等問題壓抑，導致 2027 年筆電出貨量下滑高個位數至
低雙位數左右，考慮到目前筆電營收占比已不足 15%，因此我們認為 2027
年合併營收將受伺服器的強勁成長帶動而年增。 
預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，稅後 EPS 7.19 元：預期 4Q26 筆電市場仍舊低
迷，AI 伺服器處於世代交替的轉換期，出貨量將持續下滑，但受 ASP 提升
影響而帶動 AI 伺服器營收季增，此外通用型伺服器出貨亦維持強勁。綜上

4 群益投顧 
所述，群益預估 4Q26 營收 1.19 兆元，QoQ+7.0%：毛利率 4.62%， QoQ-
0.13ppts，毛利續持續下滑主要受到 AI 伺服器 ASP 提升帶來的稀釋，以及
筆電出貨量下滑造成的規模經濟效果衰減所影響；營業利益 331.68 億元，
QoQ+3.0%，營益率 2.78%；歸屬母公司淨利 288.35 億元，QoQ+1.45%；稅
後 EPS 7.02 元。 
預估 2027 年營收 5.99 兆元，稅後 EPS 30.85 元：群益預估 2027 年合併營
收 5.99 兆元，YoY+44.1%；毛利率 4.18%，YoY-0.6ppts；營業利益 1,490.08
億元，YoY+21.5%，營益率 2.49%， YoY-0.5ppts；歸屬母公司淨利 1,266.99
億元，YoY+18.3%，稅後 EPS 30.85 元。 
投資建議：廣達目前未見主要 CSP 客戶調整 AI 伺服器訂單，且考慮到 CSP
對於記憶體、CPU 和 PCB 等關鍵零組件的鎖料能力，我們預期 Vera Rubin
將於如原先預期於 1Q27 放量，帶動 2027 年 AI 伺服器營收維持高雙位數成
長。通用型伺服器則受惠於 AI Agent 帶動運算需求及市占提升，預估全年
營收呈雙位數年增。預期伺服器強勁的成長將可抵銷筆電市場的低迷，帶動
2027 年營收年增，惟 Vera Rubin 的高單價將使毛利率及營益率承壓，但營
業利益亦將仍持續增長，整體營運展望維持正向，然而考慮到近期市場對於
資料中心建置的疑慮升溫而可能抑制評價，因此我們給予廣達 Trading Buy
的投資建議，目標價 324。

8 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。  
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

1 
2606 裕民 ( U-MING )

報告日期：2026/11/17 研 究 員：林奕頎(Yichi Lin) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
報告重點：預估 4Q / 2026 稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，YoY + 51% 
旺季，10 月營收 YoY+57%，11 月及 12 月 YoY 仍強。 
1.預估 4Q / 2026 單季稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，創最近 67 季新高 
裕民公告 2026 年 10 月 單月營收 YoY + 51%，創歷史新高。 
宏遠基於保守原則，估裕民 11 月營收 18.50 億元； 12 月營
收 19.05 億元； 估 4Q 單季稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，YoY + 51%。 
若裕民 11 月 及 12 月營收 高於預期，則須上修 4Q 獲利。 
2.BDI 處於獲利水準，裕民 2027 年增加 6 艘新船，將受惠 
2026 年 前三季 BDI 平均 2,586；裕民前三季稅後 EPS 5.80
元。 2026 年 11 月 13 日 BDI 2,980，仍處於明顯獲利水準。 
裕民 2027 年新增 6艘船：輕便型 3艘 + 海岬船 2艘 + 1 艘 
高階海事工程船（用於風力發電工程）； 裕民總船數從 2026
年 72 艘，增加至 2027 年底 78 艘。 
3.海運機構估計 2026 年供給過剩，實際上，BDI 大漲 64% 
海運機構預期 2026 年散裝航運供給過剩，但 2026 年（ 截 至 11
月 13 日）BDI 運價指數平均 2,615，遠高於 2025 年同期平均
值 1,597，高出 64%； 很明顯，海運機構的預測值，忽略某些
關鍵性因素，導致預測結果出現偏差。 
目前海運研究機構 BIMCO，預估 2027 年散裝航運供給成長率
（ + 3.5% ~ + 4.5%），將超過需求成長率（ + 0.5% ~ + 
1.5% ），延續 2026 年供給過剩的看法。 
宏遠基於保守原則，暫時採用 BIMCO 看法： 
(1)假設 2027 年 BDI 運價下跌，裕民 2027 年毛利率 + 28.8%，
將明顯低於 2026 年毛利率 + 35.3%，下滑 6.5 個百分點。 
(2)裕民依照造船計畫，2027 年新增 6 艘新船。 
宏遠估計裕民 2027 年稅後 EPS + 6.51 元，將低於 2026 年預
估值 + 8.14 元； 若 2027 年毛利率下滑幅度，低於 6.5 個百
分點，則必須上調裕民 2027 年獲利預估值。 
4.買進：26 年 EPS 創 18 年新高； 高殖利率行情，將激勵股價 
裕民 2026 年前三季稅後 EPS + 5.80 元，10 月單月營收創歷史
新高； 宏遠預估 裕民 2026 年稅後 EPS + 8.14 元，將創最近
18 年新高；同時，估計 2027 年 3 月公告每股現金股利，將介
於 4.7 ~ 6.0 元，將有高殖利率題材 ； 建議買進。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：95 元 
 1.60 X 2027 年底每股淨值

參考價位：71.6 元 
 P/B：1.21 X 2027 年底每股淨值 
 市值：605 億元 
 2026 / 11 / 16 收盤

裕民 2023 年 ~ 2025 年 
現金股利發放比重 
介於 58% ~ 74%

2026 年稅後 EPS + 8.14 元 
比照 2023 年 ~ 2025 年發放比重

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一.公司簡介 
截至 2026 年 9 月（法說資料），裕民實際經營船隻為 72 艘： 
海岬型船  25 艘：自有 22 艘 + 3 艘合資。 
巴拿馬型船 24 艘：自有 14 艘 + 7 艘代管 + 3 艘合資。 
輕便型     8艘（自有）。 
水泥船     4艘（自有）。 
油輪       4艘：透過 環能海運（持股 40%）。 
CTV 離岸風電人員運輸船 6 艘（自有 ； 裕民風能航運）。 
天然氣船   1 艘（持有 50%；與日本 K LINE 合資）。

在建船隻 14 艘，交船時程： 
2026 年：輕便型 1 艘（預期 11 月交付）。 
2027 年：輕便型 3 艘+ 海岬船 2 艘+ 高階海事工程船 1 艘。 
2028 年：輕便型 2 艘+ 海岬船 2 艘+ 高階海事工程船 1 艘。 
2030 年：海岬船 2 艘。

二.產業趨勢概況 
1.海運機構預期 2026 年供給過剩； 實際上，BDI 走勢強勁 
雖然，先前，多家海運專業研調機構，都預測 2026 年全球
散裝航運，供給成長率超過需求成長率，將出現供給過剩的
狀況。

實際上，2026 年截至 11 月 13 日，散裝 BDI 運價走勢都優於
2025 年同期表現（參閱圖 1） 。

受惠於 BDI 的強勁走勢，裕民： 
1Q / 2026 稅後 EPS + 1.17 元，優於 1Q / 2025 同期 + 0.27 元。 
2Q / 2026 稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，優於 2Q / 2025 同期 + 0.77 元。 
3Q / 2026 稅後 EPS + 2.29 元，優於 3Q / 2025 同期 + 1.72 元。 
裕民公告 2026 年 10 月 單月營收 創歷史新高。

從 BDI 的表現來看： 
1Q / 2026（過年淡季）BDI 平均 1,955。 
2Q / 2026（美伊戰爭爆發，燃煤替代天然氣）BDI 平均 2,751。 
3Q / 2026  BDI 平均 3,041。 
2026 年 11 月 13 日 單日 BDI  2,980。 
2025 年 4Q 旺季 BDI 高點 2,845。

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
1.估 2026 年稅後 EPS + 8.14 元，YoY + 89%

先前，多家國際海運研調機構，預期 2026 年散裝航運供給過
剩，但 2026 年（截至 11 月 13 日）BDI 運價指數平均 2,615，

明顯，海運機構的預測值，忽略某些關鍵性因素，導致預測結
果出現偏差。

裕民公告 1Q / 2026 財報營收 38.92 億元，YoY + 13%。 
稅後 EPS + 1.17 元，YoY + 335%。 
公告 2Q / 2026 財報營收 50.14 億元，YoY + 39%。 
稅後 EPS + 2.34 元，YoY + 204%。 
公告 3Q / 2026 財報營收 52.78 億元，YoY + 23%。 
稅後 EPS + 2.29 元，YoY + 33%。 
公告 2026 年 10 月單月營收 21.77 億元，YoY + 57%，新高。

裕民 2026 年 11 月 將下水 1艘 輕便型船。

宏遠估計，裕民 2026 年： 
營收 201.16 億元，YoY + 23%；

營業利益 61.96 億元，YoY + 67%；

業外淨收支 + 8.43 億元，YoY + 503%； 
權益法轉投資收益改善 及 金融資產評價利益增加。

稅後純益 68.75 億元，YoY + 89%；

稅後 EPS + 8.14 元，優於 2025 年財報稅後 EPS + 4.31 元。

2.估 2027 年稅後 EPS + 6.51 元，YoY - 20% 
假設：(1) 海運研調機構預測， 2027 年全球散裝航運過剩。 
2027 年 BDI 下跌。

(2) 假設 BDI 下跌，裕民 2027 年毛利率下滑 6.5 個百分點。

2026 年 11 月 下水 1艘新船； 
2027 年 下水 6艘新船； 
成為 2027 年營收上升的主因。

營業利益 54.17 億元，YoY - 13%； 
BDI 下跌，造成 毛利率 及 營利率 下滑。

業外淨收支 + 3.59 億元，YoY - 57%； 
2026 年認列金融資產評價利益。 
2027 年配合新船下水，利息費用上升。

稅後純益 55.04 億元，YoY - 20%；

稅後 EPS + 6.51 元，低於 2026 年稅後 EPS 預估值 + 8.14 元。

延續 2026 年 供給過剩看法 
預估 2027 年 持續過剩

### Page 2

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Investment Thesis: WNC 
WNC is a leading network communications solutions provider with technology across 
WiFi, Cellular, and Satellite. The company is the leading original design manufacturer 
(ODM) for major networking brands (e.g. Cisco, HP, etc.) and serves the leading US 
telecom operators (e.g. Verizon, AT&T, etc.). WNC is also the sole supplier of Starlink’s 
low earth orbits (LEO) satellites’ user terminal (power supply, antenna and router). We 
see WNC as an early beneficiary in the growing Space Economy, riding on Starlink’s 
subscribers growth, along with recovering traditional networking business driven by a 
low base in 2025, WiFi 7, 5G FWA (fixed wireless access) investments from US and 
Europe, and customers’ needs on diversifying suppliers, leading to a 14% NI CAGR in 
2026-28E. WNC is trading at 15x 2027E P/E, lower than the average of its main peers, 
which is attractive in our view. We are Buy rated on WNC. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology: WNC 
Valuation methodology: We use a near-term P/E to derive our 12m target price for 
WNC, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our TP of NT$380.0 on 
a target P/E multiple of 28.0x on a forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E is derived 
from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers. 
Key downside risks: fiercer-than-expected competition in satellite communication, 
slower-than-expected Wi-Fi 7 or 5G FWA ramp-up in US and Europe markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

Elite Material Co.  | Asia Pacific
Post-AP Summit Taiwan
Corporate Day Feedback
Substrate CCL: EMC believes its substrate CCL will be fully qualified by three ABF
substrate customers (two based in Taiwan and one in Japan), who are supplying a
key CSP's ASIC project by EoY 2026. It is now undergoing reliability test with these
three customers and their OSAT partners.
Share gains: EMC believes it has become the sole CCL supplier for a key GPU

customer's current-gen switch board (32L PCB on EM-892K2 CCL) after taking share
from a key competitor. This echoes our supply chain checks  in our Nov 11 report.
New projects: EMC noted it is the major, if not sole, CCL supplier for two key
projects, 1) M8 CCLs for  Starmind and 2) M10 CCLs for the midplane PCB in Kessel
AI racks. It expects to start shipping CCLs for the Starmind project as early as 2Q27
and to enter volume production for its M10 CCLs in CY27.

Material shortage: EMC expects the thin E glass cloth to remain in short supply in
CY27, though it believes the 7628-type E glass shortage will ease in 1H27, echoing
what Nanya Ya New Material (not covered) mentioned at our AP Summit last week
(link).

Key Takeaways
EMC now has 300k SPM of ready substrate CCL capacity and plans  to reach 1.2M
SPM by EoY 2027.
EMC expects high-end HVLP copper foils to be the most under-supplied heading
into CY27, despite having six copper foil suppliers.
EMC expects the thin E glass cloth to remain in short supply in CY27, though it
believes the 7628-type E glass shortage will ease in 1H27.

For low Dk and Q glass, EMC believes it has secured sufficient supply to support
growth in CY27.
EMC expects to maintain 40%+ GM in CY27, as M9/10 and substrate CCLs ramp
in larger volumes and are margin-accretive.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

Source PDF: all report pdf/[000660] SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것SK하이닉스(000660) 실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것실적은 우려를 불식시킬 것 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
좋은 실적이 상수인 구간 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다. 이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 
이전 전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 26년 3분기 
영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 상회한 
수준이다. 
실적이 끌고, 수급이 밀고 
지금까지는 높은 실적에 대한 불안보다는 먼 미래에 대한 불확실성을 앞서 주가에 
반영시키는 힘이 더 강하게 작용하다가, 주가가 1,190,000원을 찍은 이후 상승세를 
보이고 있다. 실적을 발표한 10월 28일도 10% 넘게 상승했다. 기대 이상의 실적과 
미국 반도체 시장 분위기가 좋았던 영향이다. 앞으로는 악재에 대해서 민감도가 떨
어지는 구간에 진입할 것으로 전망한다. 지나치게 하락한 주가와 밸류에이션이 버팀
목이 될 것이고, Anthropic의 IPO까지 발표되는 Big Tech 기업들의 실적은 시장의 
우려를 충분히 상쇄할 수 있을 것으로 기대한다. 추가로 진행되는 자사주 매수도 불
안한 수급을 해소할 수 있을 전망이다. 
목표주가는 괴리율을 고려해서 하향 조정 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. 26년에도 AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 
시장은 성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 
때문이다. 26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 
판단한다. 목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 
업황에 대한 우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 27년 EPS 
538,187원에 PER 6배를 적용하였다.

 SK하이닉스 (000660)

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660)

2 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 3분기, 모든 악재를 잠재운 실적 
SK하이닉스의 26년 3분기 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 31.9% 증가한 104조 
6,150억원이다.  이전 전망 대비 증가한 수준이다. DRAM, NAND 관련 데이터는 이전 
전망 대비 물량은 유사했지만 가격 상승폭은 예상보다 높았다. 
DRAM 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 35.4% 증가했다. B/G는 +11.5%, ASP는 
+28.2%로 추정했다. 3분기 DRAM 가격 상승률은 경쟁사 대비 높은 수준이다. Server 
DRAM 비중이 2분기 대비 높아진 것과 고객 믹스가 개선된 영향으로 추정한다. 
HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정한다. HBM이 3분기 가격에 미친 영향은 
제한적인 수준으로 판단한다.  
NAND 매출액은 26년 2분기 대비 18.2% 증가했다. B/G는 -1%, ASP는 +26%로 
추정한다. 이전 전망치를 상회했다. SSD 비중은 2분기 75%에서 3분기에는 77%로 
상승했다.  
26년 3분기 영업이익은 26년 2분기 대비 37.6% 증가한 83.3조원이다. 이전 전망을 
상회한 수준이다. DRAM 영업이익은 지난 분기 대비 42.3% 증가한 65.1조원으로 
예상한다. 영업이익률은 전 분기 대비 4.0%p 상승한 83.0%로 추정한다. NAND 
영업이익은 전 분기 대비 23.1% 증가한 18.2조원으로 추정한다. 영업이익률은 전 
분기 대비 2.9%p 높아진 72.4%로 추정한다.

4 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
26년 4분기, DRAM 물량 효과 
SK하이닉스의 26년 4분기 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 29.4% 증가한 135.3조원으로 
예상한다. 가격상승은 지속될 것으로 예상하지만 26년 3분기 대비 상승폭이 낮아질 
전망이다. 물량은 3분기 대비 25% 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 신규 라인 가동 영향이다. 
4분기 영업이익은 26년 3분기 대비 34.1% 증가한 111.7조원으로 예상한다. DRAM 
90.9조원, NAND 20.7조원이다.  
DRAM 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 36.4% 증가한 107조원으로 예상한다. ASP는 
+15%, Bit Growth는 +25.0%로 가정하였다. HBM 출하는 지난 분기 대비 증가할 
것으로 예상하고, HBM4 비중은 60%대로 높아질 전망이다. 
26년 4분기 NAND 매출액은 26년 3분기 대비 11.6% 증가할 것으로 예상한다. Bit 
Growth는 +5%로 전망하고, ASP는 +12%로 예상한다. SSD 비중은 소폭 개선될 
전망이다. SSD 내에서 eSSD 비중은 90% 수준이다.

IBKS RESEARCH │5 
공정 전환 속도는 예상보다 빠르게 진행 중: 1cb nm, 321L 
SK하이닉스는 1c nm 비중을 빠르게 확대하고 있다. Wafer 투입이 제한된 상황에서 
공급 확대를 위한 최선의 방법이기 때문이다. HBM 수요는 1b nm를 중심으로 대응할 
예정이다. 이와 관련된 물량도 꾸준히 증가하지만, 출하 Bit 기준으로 1c nm 비중이 
26년 1분기 3% 수준에서 26년 3분기에는 20%, 연말에는 33%까지 높아질 전망이다. 
HBM이 아닌 DRAM 물량이 크게 증가할 전망이다. 이를 통해서 높아진 DRAM 수요 
대응할 것으로 예상한다. 26년 출하 Bit Growth는 28.5%로 상향되었다. 
NAND 생산라인의 공정 전환은 High-End로 꾸준히 변화 중이다. 321L는 26년 
2분기에는 176L와 Cross over 되었다. 예상보다 1분기 빠른 속도다. 26년 3분기 321L 
비중은 50%이다. 26년말까지 321L를 64%까지 확대할 계획이다.

6 │ IBKS RESEARCH 
Check Point 
1. DRAM ASP의 차별화 
26년 3분기 DRAM ASP는 메이커별로 차별화가 크다. 26년 2분기 대비 상승폭은 마
이크론이 10% 후반, 삼성전자는 20% 초반으로 추정하고, SK하이닉스는 20% 후반이
다. LTA, HBM 등 다양한 변수가 있지만 SK하이닉스는 고객 비중 변화가 가장 큰 변
수라고 설명한다.  
2. HBM4 물량, 수율 문제 
26년 3분기에 가장 시끄러웠던 변수가 SK하이닉스의 HBM4 관련 품질 문제이다. 26
년 3분기 DRAM 매출과 영업이익으로는 문제가 있었다고 판단하기 어렵다. 26년 3분
기 HBM에서 HBM4 비중은 30%를 상회한 것으로 추정되고 4분기 말에는 비중이 60%
까지 높아질 것으로 전망한다.   
3. HBM 8Hi 확대가 시장 규모 감소는 아니다. 
HBM4는 12Hi가 원래 제품 사양이다. HBM 공급이 부족해지면서 GPU를 좀 더 많이 
팔기 위해서 적용되는 HBM 용량을 줄이는 방향으로 제품 설계가 변경되는 중이다. 
2027년 하반기에는 8Hi 비중이 12Hi 비중을 상회할 것으로 예상한다. 하지만 이로 
인해서 HBM 전체 물량이 감소하는 것은 아닌 것으로 전망하고 있다.  
4. 투자와 생산은 다르다 
SK하이닉스는 용인팹을 공기를 앞당겨서 완공할 계획이다. 이로 인해서 공급량이 예
상 대비 커지는 것에 대한 우려가 있지만 건물 완공과 장비 반입에는 시차가 있고, 수
요를 고려하지 않은 장비 반입은 없을 것으로 밝혔다. 
5. 주주환원. 더 남아 있다 
공시를 통해서 26년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원의 주주환원 계획을 밝혔다. 배당을 30
조원 이상, 자사주 매입/소각을 60조원이상을 추진할 계획이다. 26년 4분기 중에 배
당은 14조원을 집행하고, 자사주는 20조원을 매입할 예정이다. 자사주 20조원 중에서 
10조원은 종업원 상여금 지급용이어서 주주환원에 포함되지 않는다.  
26년 4분기 실적 발표에서 추가적인 배당과 자사주 매입에 대한 계획을 발표할 것으
로 예상한다.

ASP 움직임은 메이커별로

IBKS RESEARCH │7 
펀더멘탈과 센티멘탈의 줄다리기 구간 
SK하이닉스에 대한 투자의견은 매수를 유지한다. AI 서버 중심으로 메모리 시장은 
성장할 것으로 기대하고, 실적 대비 주가는 저평가 국면에 있다고 판단하기 때문이다. 
26년 급격히 하락한 주가는 업황, 펀더멘탈을 설명하기에는 과한 움직임이라 판단한다.  
목표주가는 4,000,000원에서 3,200,000원으로 하향한다. 메모리 시장의 업황에 대한 
우려가 아니라 현재 주가와의 괴리율을 고려한 수준이다. 2027년 EPS 538,187원에 PER 
6배를 적용하였다.  
아직 제대로 보급되지 않은 Agent AI가 확산되면 컴퓨팅 파워 필요성에 대한 인식도 
크게 바뀔 것으로 전망한다. 메타의 MUSE는 가입자당 8GB의 DRAM을 가정하고 
있다. Open AI, Google, Anthropic에서도 Agent Platform이 공개된다면 필요한 
DRAM은 폭발적으로 증가할 것으로 전망한다. 
메모리 수요에 대한 규모가 너무 커지는 것이 걱정이지 누가 살 것인지에 대한 것은 
걱정할 문제가 아니라 판단한다. 기존 AI 모델의 진화, 효율적 모델의 등장, Agent AI 
모델의 확대 모두 메모리 수요를 기하급수적으로 확장시키는 변수이다. 
투자 주체들의 자금난이 수요를 저해하는 변수는 아닌 것으로 판단한다. 대체 
투자자들은 지속적으로 증가할 것으로 전망하기 때문이다.  
메모리 업체들의 투자 계획은 수요에 대응하기 위한 투자일 뿐 공급과잉을 만들 
수준은 아닐 것으로 판단한다.

동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

두산에너빌리티 (034020)

Company Brief 
유틸리티/건설 
Analyst 문경원, CFA 
kyeongwon.moon@meritz.co.kr 
RA 이동석 
dongseok.lee@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 Review: 바닥을 잡는다면 지금  
믹스 개선에 따른 이익률 개선 조금씩 관찰 중 
3Q26 연결 영업이익은 2,612억원(+90.6% YoY)을 기록하며 컨센서스(2,455억원)
를 소폭 상회했다. 밥캣(1,674억원)이 컨센서스(1,761억원)를 소폭 하회한 가운데, 
에너빌리티 부문 영업이익(+179% YoY, OPM 6.6%)이 호실적을 이끌었다. 환율 하
락에도 불구, 가스터빈, 원전 등 고수익성 프로젝트의 비중 상승이 주요 원인이다. 
3Q26 누적 수주는 10.0조원(+85.5% YoY)을 기록했다. 4Q26 가스복합 EPC 수주 
등을 감안하면 연간 가이던스(13.3조원) 달성은 무난하다. 한수원과의 협상 결렬로 
이목이 집중된 체코 두코바니 시공 수주는 2027년 인식될 확률이 높아 보인다. 주간
사가 바뀌더라도 이미 프로젝트에 참여 중인 동사를 배제하기는 쉽지 않다.

대미투자 이후, 연말~연초 수주 모멘텀에 집중 
연말 연초에 다음과 수주 모멘텀이 집중되면서, 주가 상승을 이끌 전망이다. 
1) 대형원전: 정부 자금 중심의 Westinghouse LLI(장납기 기자재) 발주가 몰린다. 
우선 미국 DOE(에너지부)가 제공한 대출을 기반으로 미국 수주가 기대된다.  폴란드 
정부 역시 최근 LLI 발주 작업을 시작했다. 한편, 한국 정부는 연말까지 100억 달러 
규모의 대미 투자 송금을 협의 중인데, 이 역시 DOC(상무부) 주도의 대형 원전 건설
을 위한 LLI 발주에 쓰일 가능성이 높다 
2) SMR: 오래 기다려왔던 Nuscale 향 수주가 시작되고 있다. 우선 29일 모듈 1기 
제작 계약을 체결했다. 현금이 부족한 Nuscale이 용기를 냈다는 건, 곧 Nuscale-
TVA 간 PPA 가 임박했음을 의미한다. 본격적인 수주는 PPA 직후 예상된다.  
3) 가스 터빈: 2026년 초 해외 수주(7기)는 2029년까지 인도 계획이다. 이후 2030
년 슬랏 예약을 완료하고 2031년 물량을 논의 중으로 보인다. 지금은 2030년 슬랏
에 대한 PO가 나와 수주잔고에 인식할 타이밍이다. 역시 수출 물량으로 추측한다.

성장에 대한 기대가 사라진 밸류에이션, 지금이 바닥 
투자의견 Buy를 유지, 할인율 상승을 반영하여 적정주가를 109,000원으로 -17% 
하향 조정한다. 금리 상승, 환율 하락, 트럼프 지지율 하락 등 Top-down 리스크에 
최근 조정을 받았지만, 추가적인 주가 하락 폭은 제한적일 전망이다. EV/선행 수주잔
고 지표는 현재 1.0배 수준까지 하락하며, 원전의 성장성이 주목받지 못했던 
2021~2024년 수준에 근접하게 내려왔다.  
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 30

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 109,000원 
현재주가 (10.29) 56,700원 
상승여력 92.2%

### Page 1

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
실적&임단협 우려 해소, 발전엔진 수요 폭발적 
2026-10-29

조선/방산. 한승한, R.A 고서영 / shane.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-9992

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 640,000 원(하향) 
현재주가: 285,500 원 
상승여력: 124.2%

Signal: 3Q26, 조업일수 감소 및 환율 하락으로 시장 예상치 하회 
Key: 4Q26~1Q27 부터 본격적인 발전엔진 및 FDC 수주 모멘텀 전망 
Step: 상선 우려 감안해도 엔진&FDC 으로 밸류 매력도 충분. 매수 기회

3Q26 Review: 시장예상치 하회하는 실적 시현  
3Q26 연결 매출액 5조 7,536억원(+30.2% YoY, -9.1% QoQ), 영업이익 8,992억원
(+61.3% YoY, -13.5% QoQ)으로, 시장 예상치(9,792억원)를 하회했다. 조업일수 감소
(휴가 10 일+중대재해 4일) 및 환율 하락 영향(450 억원)과 더불어, 임단협 관련 예상 비
용 중 75%를 이번 분기 선반영 했기 때문이다. 상선 수주 빈티지는 중형 선박 기준 ‘23 년 
9.6%, ‘24년 65%, ‘25년 25%, 대형 선박 기준 ‘22년 4%, ‘23년 45%, ‘24년 31%, 
‘25년 20%로, 선가 상승효과 지속되는 중이다. 특히 해양 부문에서 루야 프로젝트의 공정
률 확대 효과 및 C/O로 180억원의 환입 발생으로 전 분기 대비 큰 폭의 이익 성장 시현했
다. 최근 키윗과 미국 내 합작 조선소 설립을 추진하며, 미 군수지원함 초도 2척 수주 및 향
후 물량 건조, 그리고 HII와의 함정 선체블록 하도급 사업 수주까지 기대할 수 있다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 유지, 목표주가 64 만원으로 하향 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’를 유지하고, 목표주가는 기존 82 만원에서 64 만원
으로 하향한다. 최근 상선 실적 피크아웃 우려에 따른 조선주 주가 급락으로 멀티플 하향 조
정했다. 하지만 상선 부문의 우려를 감안하더라도 상선 사이클과 무관한 육상 발전 엔진 및 
FDC 에 대한 글로벌 하이퍼스케일러들의 수요 확대는 지속되고 있는 중이다. 올해 공시 기
준으로 육상발전 엔진 약 1.6GW 수주한 것으로 알려졌으나, 미공시된 약 1.4GW 까지 포
함 시 총 3.0GW 이상을 수주한 상황이다. 캐파 증설 기준으로 ‘28 년 1.5GW, ‘29 년 
3GW 캐파에 해당하는 물량을 이미 대부분 채웠으며, 기존 계획 대비 빠른 준공 혹은 공정
률 확대, 더 나아가 추가적인 캐파 증설까지 고려할 수준으로 파악된다. 빠르면 올해 연말 
혹은 내년 초부터 4 행정 중속엔진&FDC 본격 수주를 통해 중장기적 신성장 동력 확보할 
전망이다. 현 주가 레벨에서 상승여력 충분히 남아있다는 판단으로, 3Q 실적 및 임단협 불
확성 해소된 현 시점에서 적극적 매수 의견 및 조선주 최선호주(Top-Pick)로 제시한다.

본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Unemployment and output both show a common pattern
of downturns being short, sharp and irregular, while the
subsequent recoveries and expansions are longer and
slower. The eminent economist Milton Friedman likened
this pattern to plucking on a string. The causes are unclear,

though there are some plausible candidates.
• If we take the “plucking” pattern seriously, there are clear
policy implications. Standard models used by central banks
do not allow for this pattern and therefore systematically
overstate how often capacity is tight. And when a
downturn does occur, the “plucking” view argues for
forceful stabilisation policy.
• We believe the current slow upward grind in
unemployment in Australia is a different phenomenon

and expect slow growth rather than a sharp downturn. If
people start thinking current inflation pressures demand a
1980s-style “Volcker disinflation”, though, it could just end
up plucking the economy.
Look at a graph of the unemployment rate for almost any
western economy, and you will notice a few things. The
big increases are sharp and sudden, while the subsequent
recoveries are slow and last longer. The timing of those upward
spikes is irregular, with no obvious pattern. The height of the

spike in unemployment is a good predictor of how far the rate
declines in the recovery that follows. By contrast, neither the
length of an expansion phase of declining unemployment, nor
the size of the decline predict the size of the next downturn.
This pattern is a far cry from the notion of a business
cycle, where output and unemployment oscillate round
some average or trend. There is something different about
downturns. They are not just like expansions but a bit slower.

The eminent economist Milton Friedman described this as a
“plucking” model. The economy, he suggested, was a bit like a
guitar string. Most of the time it stayed close to the fretboard.
There is a physical maximum amount of production possible
at any one time. Occasionally, though, the economy could be
plucked away from that maximum, with output falling and
unemployment rising sharply.
Remarkably, the mainstream macroeconomic literature that
central banks rely on has engaged little with this feature of the

data. Forecasting models used by the RBA and its peers do not
produce this pattern. Some authors have looked at “plucking”-
type models, though. Their analysis confirmed that downturns
predict the size of the following expansion, but the reverse
Is the economy ‘plucked’?
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
is not true. Expansions do not “die of old age” by building up
imbalances. Australia went for 30 years without a proper
downturn: it turns out that this is entirely possible provided

nothing bad enough happens.
These authors further argue that the maximum production level
during expansions is buffeted by small shocks, some temporary,
others positive and permanent – think new technologies and
ways of doing things, or a policy change that encourages
workforce participation. Downturns, by contrast, are triggered
by large shocks that are usually temporary and arrive at irregular
intervals. Some argue that it is best to think of economies as

having three phases: normal expansions, which can continue
for long periods; shorter-lived downturns; and subsequent
recovery phases where growth can outstrip normal rates and
unemployment declines, but at a slower pace than it rose.
Some downturns do not end in full recovery, however. This
observation links to another strand of research showing that
the output losses associated with financial crises and wars are
not fully recovered. The experience after the Global Financial

Crisis shows this pattern in a number of advanced economies.
Standard models would interpret this as slower growth in
potential output, perhaps feeding the Australian tradition of
hand-wringing about insufficient productivity or reform.
While the pattern in the data is clear, the causes of “plucking”
are far from settled, with some authors seeing boom–bust
dynamics as inherent in the economic system rather than
driven by shocks. Some recent papers – including preliminary

RBA work – look to certain frictions in the labour market
for an explanation. These include search frictions, which
arise because workers and firms sometimes require time to
find a good match with each other. Another at least partial
explanation is that workers resist absolute wage cuts but
accept falls in purchasing power when dollar wages are
constant in the face of positive inflation.
An objection to this line of research is the finding by some BIS

researchers that the plucking pattern is clearer in countries
with more flexible labour markets – mostly English-speaking
ones. The literature focusing on labour market frictions also
does not explain where the originating pluck comes from,
“Downturns are usually short, sharp and
arrive at irregular intervals; expansions are
longer and slower."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
only why the data have the dynamics they do when those
shocks occur. Something must be causing the “pluck” shock,
if indeed it is a shock. The BIS paper suggests one possibility:
the authors find that output in the manufacturing and trade
sectors shows a clearer pluck pattern than construction.

Construction downturns tend to linger, perhaps because of
their connections to indebtedness and financial crises.
If we take seriously the patterns in the data showing
asymmetric downturns and the plucking pattern Friedman
described, there are clear policy implications. Currently, the
models underlying central bank forecasts are symmetric by
construction. They therefore systematically understate spare
capacity during expansions. They will find a positive output

gap ahead of a downturn, even when there was no evidence of
overheating at the time. This oversight risks policy mistakes.
The asymmetric nature of downturns also means that the
benefits of macroeconomic stabilisation policy are mostly
about cushioning downturns and hastening recoveries. In
normal times, the benefits will be small.
We also note that the RBA paper mentioned above hypothesised
a short-run NAIRU and a long-run NAIRU that are both pushed

around by actual unemployment in a path-dependent pattern.
This is an argument for “holding on to the employment gains”
that may have been missed by people whose mental model of
the economy is fully symmetric, without any plucking.
The plucking model started from empirical observations
about large downturns. The kind of slow grind upwards in
unemployment seen in Australia in the 2010s and in the past
couple of years does not fit this pattern. It is a qualitatively

different phenomenon requiring other explanations, including
perhaps the path-dependent drag upwards of the feasible
unemployment rate by the actual unemployment rate.
It is these kinds of considerations that underlie our current
forecast for slow growth and not a downturn, even though this
week’s labour market data were concerning. A further 25 basis
points or so of rate increases is unlikely to be the thing that
causes a severe downturn. Typically, downturns are not just

slower expansions but are qualitatively different phases that
require a trigger.
The one thing that makes me nervous is that the plucking
literature finds that every downturn is different. The famous
opening line to Tolstoy’s Anna Karenina, “Happy families are
all alike; every unhappy family is unhappy in its own way,”
would seem to apply to economies as well. And in at least one
case, a forceful tightening of monetary policy was the trigger
for a downturn. The so-called “Volcker disinflation” brought

US inflation under control in the early 1980s. That episode is
widely regarded as a success, despite the output costs. In the
current circumstances, some policymakers and commentators
might be tempted to believe that the same medicine is needed;
nobody wants inflation expectations to become entrenched,
after all. However, as RBA Board member Ian Ross has noted,
the institutions of the 1970s that produced wage–price spirals
are no longer operative. So there is no guarantee that a similar

approach nowadays would be necessary or as effective.
Watch out then, if the discourse turns to arguments that
countries “need a recession” to get inflation under control. A
Volcker-style policy could just end up plucking the economy.
Forecast changes
• We have upgraded our WPI forecast following this week's data
release, with our year-end forecast now 3.4%yr.
• We have also upgraded our near-term unemployment rate,
with a year-end forecast of 4.9%.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: risks mount
risks are skewed to the upside. FOMC members who spoke
throughout the week also kept the focus on price risks, and called
for the FOMC to act swiftly. Governor Waller again proposed a
50bp hike in December; others seemingly favour a 25bp hike at

the December and January meetings, as Westpac forecasts, and
continuing to tighten further in 2027 if necessary.
Very clearly, the labour market and activity growth are not
of concern for US policy makers. Retail sales gave further
justification for this view this week, the control group
surprising to the upside with a 0.6% gain in October after
a material upward revision to September (0.5%). That said,
housing is under considerable pressure, pending home sales

falling 17.1% over the past year, and housing starts back at
2019 levels despite a 9.9% rebound in October. Persistent
weakness in this sector will, in time, have implications for both
consumption and investment.
China’s October data round was the other key international
development of the week. Again, it provided a call for pro-
active stimulus in scale. In the month, annual retail sales
growth almost stalled at 0.4%yr and the decline in fixed
asset investment broadened outside property, with capital

expenditure now down 8.1% across the economy year-to-
date. Industrial production again highlighted that its output to
satiate global demand which is holding up aggregate growth;
though, even here, momentum softened in October, annual
growth in production slowing from 5.5%yr to 4.8%yr. It is not
only activity that remains under pressure, but also wealth,
with new / existing home prices 0.15% / 0.24% lower in the
month. As we continue to emphasise, to prove effective,

stimulus needs to affect change in both activity and wealth
and do so in a sustainable manner.
Australia’s labour market disappointed again in October,
employment falling 43.5k following September’s 36.4k
decline. On a three-month average basis, job gains over the
year have now slowed to 1.1%yr, materially below current
growth in the working-age population, 1.7%yr. As a result, the
unemployment rate rose to a new post-pandemic high of 4.9%
despite the participation rate falling another 0.2ppts to 66.6%.

While employment growth’s descent initially reflected softer
momentum in hiring, now we are seeing evidence of a material
increase in redundancies. Most affected workers look to have
remained in employment to date, but persistent job shedding
raises the risk of a faster and more persistent uptrend in the
unemployment rate, potentially above our baseline peak
forecast of 5.2% (at mid-2027) and most certainly before the
late-2028 peak of 5.1% the RBA expects.

As such, the robust 1.1% (3.5%yr) rise in the Q3 Wage Price
Index warrants close scrutiny. The Q3 increase was skewed
towards those on awards and enterprise arrangements – pay
outcomes directly tied to the Fair Work Commission’s decision
and collective bargaining. Individual arrangements meanwhile,
the most responsive to current and expected labour market
conditions, accounted for their smallest share of a Q3 wage
increase since 2019. Business survey detail also suggests labour

cost growth will normalise quickly after the award increase.
Deteriorating economic conditions are also increasingly evident
in housing. The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index fell 2.2%
to 83.2 in November, among the weakest readings since 2005.
Interestingly, there remains considerable latent demand: almost
13% of consumers intend to purchase a home over the next
year, and relatively few plan to sell. For now, though, weak
sentiment, high interest rates and expectations of further price

declines are keeping would-be buyers on the sidelines. These
factors also pose risks to the broader growth story.
In the US, abstracting from the Anthropic IPO, the outlook for
inflation remained the critical theme. The October FOMC minutes
highlighted that inflation is currently being supported by tariffs
and energy prices as well as the AI buildout, with "several
[members] judg[ing] that price pressures were increasingly
extending beyond industries directly affected by data center

construction”. Participants "assessed that medium- and longer-
term inflation expectations continued to be consistent with the
Committee's 2 percent objective", although persistent above-
target inflation is a threat to the status quo, and perceived
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“Australia’s labour market delivered a second
consecutive shock in October, pushing annual
employment growth well below working-age

population growth."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Sunny side up
This week we published our final Economic Overview
for 2026. Titled “Sunny side up”, the report shows that
New Zealand’s economic recovery is continuing. Activity
expanded by 2.6% in the year to June, and recent indicators

point to further moderate growth. Overall, GDP is forecast
to grow by 2.8% in both 2027 and 2028. But this remains a
two-speed recovery: export-facing industries and regional
centres are enjoying stronger incomes, while households
and businesses tied to the major metropolitan centres are
still contending with high inflation, a soft labour market and
a subdued housing market.
The global backdrop remains broadly supportive. On an export-
weighted basis, New Zealand’s trading partners are forecast

to grow by 3.1% in 2026, 2.9% in 2027 and 2.8% in 2028.
Stronger growth in the United States and East Asia outside
China, helped by the continuing artificial intelligence investment
buildout, is offsetting some of the drag from higher energy
prices. The central assumption underpinning these forecasts is
that a Middle East agreement eventually restores safe passage
through the Strait of Hormuz, allowing Brent crude to fall
towards US$77 a barrel in late 2027 and US$71 in late 2028.

Against that backdrop, the external sector is the clear bright
spot. Merchandise export volumes are estimated to have risen
by more than 4% this year, with dairy, meat and horticulture
joined by stronger output of manufactured metals, machinery
and equipment. Favourable growing conditions, high world
prices and a low New Zealand dollar have driven this growth.
Services exports are performing even more strongly and
are on track to grow by around 9% this year. Foreign visitor

arrivals continue to recover strongly, with Australians and
Chinese accounting for about two-thirds of the increase
seen this year. In 12-month rolling terms, visitor numbers
are expected to soon move above their 2019 peak, while
other services exports will likely continue their longer-run
outperformance.
Agriculture is expected to remain a major source of strength.
Farmgate returns are expected to stay elevated despite high
energy and shipping costs and an expected modest appreciation

in the New Zealand dollar. Meat export prices should remain
high in real terms over the next couple of years, with beef
supported by firm US demand. Kiwifruit and apple prices are
also expected to remain strong, while log prices should be
broadly stable to slightly firmer in 2027. Dairy prices – at least
powders – appear to have received an additional lift from El
Niño-related drought risk and strong global demand for protein.
We have raised our forecast milk price for the current season

by 50 cents to $10.25 per kilogram of milk solids – still ahead
of Fonterra’s own forecast, which was revised up to a $10.10
midpoint this week. We have also lifted our 2027/28 season
forecast to $9.75, reflecting a more gradual expected rise in the
New Zealand dollar than foreseen previously.
At this point, the strength offshore has not yet translated
into an equally convincing recovery for households. Average
household earnings grew only 2.9% in the year to June, while

high inflation pushed real wage growth into negative territory.
Real per capita household spending was flat over the year, and
weak house prices have limited growth in household wealth.
Spending should improve gradually during 2027 as export
income circulates through the economy, employment recovers
and inflation eases. Even so, the strongest gains are likely to
remain in regions directly exposed to agriculture and tourism.
The labour market has been unusually slow to respond to

improving GDP. Unemployment has climbed to an 11-year high
of 5.7%, and employment growth has failed to keep pace with
population growth. The pattern is best described as “low-
fire, low-hire”: firms avoided the scale of layoffs that might
normally accompany a downturn but may now have enough
staff to accommodate the early stages of a recovery without
immediately stepping up recruitment. Employment appears
to have begun turning higher and hiring intentions have

recovered, but unemployment is unlikely to decline materially
until next year. We expect it to ease to 5.1% by the end of
2027 and 4.5% in 2028.
Housing remains a restraint. National house prices have fallen
about 1.7% this year, sales have weakened and properties are
taking longer to sell. Adjusted for inflation, house prices are on
course for a fourth consecutive annual decline, and we expect
them to fall by around 33% between the 2021 peak and the

end of 2027. That adjustment is improving affordability and
supporting first-home buyers, but it also encourages existing
owners to save rather than spend. As with other economic
indicators, regional differences remain pronounced, with areas
benefiting from agricultural and tourism income outperforming
the major centres.
Residential construction should nevertheless rise during
2027, supported by a pipeline of more than 40,000 consented
homes, much of it medium-density development in Auckland

and Canterbury. The durability of that upswing is uncertain,
however, because financing costs, building costs and weak
house prices remain significant obstacles. Commercial
construction is closer to finding a floor, although office and
retail development is likely to stay subdued for a while given
elevated vacancy rates.
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Business investment has been more resilient. Capital equipment
spending rose strongly in the June quarter, and imports point to
further gains despite higher freight costs and a weaker currency.
Confidence rebounded after the initial shock from the Middle

East conflict and has held up despite renewed oil price pressure.
Investment will remain uneven, but cash-rich agricultural
businesses are well placed, and computing equipment and
software are contributing to growth.
Inflation is the main complication. Consumer prices rose 3.7%
in the year to September and annual inflation is expected to
reach 4.3% in December. Higher global oil prices have played
an important role, but the problem is broader than fuel. Core

inflation measures remain around 3%, operating costs are
elevated and firms’ pricing intentions have strengthened.
Headline inflation should fall as energy prices ease and could
temporarily dip below 2% in 2028, but underlying inflation is
expected to decline much more slowly.
That persistence means the RBNZ will likely need to tighten
policy by more than it currently forecasts. The OCR is expected
to reach 3% by year-end, but we think the neutral rate may

be closer to 3.75%, in contrast to the RBNZ’s 3% to 3.5%
estimate. As growth strengthens and core inflation proves
sticky, we expect the OCR to peak at 4% in September 2027
and remain there for about a year. Medium- and long-term
interest rates are therefore likely to settle above the unusually
low levels seen in the decade before the pandemic.
Finally, fiscal conditions should improve as stronger nominal
activity and above-target inflation lift tax revenue. However,

spending pressures and higher debt-servicing costs will be
difficult to avoid. We expect the operating balance before
gains and losses, excluding ACC (OBEGALx), to return to
surplus in 2028/29, while net core Crown debt peaks near
43.7% of GDP in 2027/28. The unresolved election adds
uncertainty, however; the forecasts assume no major near-
term change in policy direction.
Turning to this week’s key economic news, RBNZ Governor
Anna Breman and fellow MPC member Prasanna Gai spoke

to audiences in New Zealand and Australia respectively. We
thought that Breman’s comments were reasonably balanced.
On the hawkish side, Breman noted that some indicators of
economic activity had been stronger than expected. Perhaps
of greater note, she also said that this month’s partial October
CPI data (discussed below) indicated that indirect impacts
from the fuel price shock might be growing following a more
muted than expected impact in the September quarter. On

the dovish side, she noted subdued domestic demand, which
she said was making it difficult for firms to pass on higher
costs. She also referred to weak labour market data (including
weak wage growth) and the stability seen in medium-term
inflation expectations (also discussed below). Following the
October review Breman had commented that the chances of a
December rate hike were “high”.
In our view, Gai’s commentary leaned more on the dovish
side. He reiterated his concerns about the implications for NZ

of a bursting of the AI bubble and described recent inflation
and inflation expectations data as “quiescent”. In response to
a question, he indicated that he considered that news since
the October meeting had validated his decision to vote for a
pause at that meeting. The general tone of Gai’s commentary
suggested it would not be a surprise if he voted to extend the
pause at next month’s meeting. Even if he were to vote for
a hike, it seems likely that Gai would regard this as simply a

tentative step towards the roughly 3.25% neutral rate that the
Bank projected in the September Monetary Policy Statement.
On the data front, the partial CPI data for October pointed
to weaker than expected food price inflation but firmer than
expected increases in travel costs. The latter was probably
responsible for Breman’s comments above, but given the
month-to-month volatility of this data we would be hesitant
to read too much into one month’s outcome. These opposing

impacts netted out and so the outcome was consistent
with our forecast for a lift in annual inflation to 4.3% in the
December quarter. Meanwhile, the RBNZ’s long-running
survey of professional forecasters reported a lift in year-ahead
inflation expectations. However, encouragingly, the closely
watched 2-year-ahead measure was little changed at 2.36%.
Moreover, 5-year-ahead and 10-year-ahead expectations –
which had been singled out for special attention by RBNZ

Chief Economist Conway in a recent speech – fell to 2.17%
and 2.13% respectively. In both cases, these were the
lowest readings in almost two years. Finally, encouragingly,
the BusinessNZ services PMI continued its gradual trend
improvement in October, rising to a 3-year high of 51.7.

### Page 7

We expect this positive momentum to have carried into Q3 
2026, with further solid gains in residential building, private 
non-residential building and private engineering construction, 
particularly electricity-related projects. Public infrastructure 
investment will offset some of this strength, with the drag that 
began last year continuing as major projects reach completion.
AUS: October CPI (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.4%  
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.6
We expect the October CPI to rise 0.41%mth with the annual 
pace lifting from 4.3%yr to 4.8%yr, the fastest pace in the 
short history. Westpac expects this to be the peak in current 
acceleration in annual inflation. Our seasonally adjusted CPI 
estimate lifts 0.7%mth, a small step up from the 0.6% increase 
in September, highighting the seasonal softness in the month. 
Transport makes the largest contribution (+0.35ppts), almost 
entirely due to auto fuel, with smaller contributions from 
clothing & footwear (+0.06ppts), household goods & services 
(+0.05ppts) and meals out & takeaway (+0.04ppts). The 
main offsets are recreation & culture (–0.18ppts) with fruit & 
vegetables, utilities and others providing marginal offsets.
For more details please see “October CPI Preview“.  
AUS: October Trimmed Mean (%mth)
November 25, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.4% 
Mkt f/c: 0.4%, Range : 0.3 to 0.5
Our October Trimmed Mean estimate is 0.42%mth, a step up from 
0.3% in September, taking the annual pace to 3.7%yr from 3.6%yr 
and lifting the six month annualised pace to 4.2%yr, from 4.0%yr, 
which is also the fastest pace in the short history of the series. 
Rents and dwellings continue to underpin the TM estimates. 
We see risks as slightly tilted to the upside. Our estimates 
allow for a greater degree of price resetting in areas such as 
meals out & takeaway and personal and household services, 
but this adjustment proves broader than our assumptions. 
Fuel levy surcharges are another source of upside risk, 
including increases by Woolworths and Australia Post. The 
key downside risk remains housing, particularly new dwelling 
prices, which surprised to the downside in recent months.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 retail spending (%qtr)
Nov 23, Last: -0.5, Westpac: 1.4
The June quarter saw a larger than expected 0.5% fall in
retail spending, with high fuel prices squeezing households’
spending power. However, even with the drag from high
fuel prices, core spending (excl. fuels and vehicles) remained

resilient, rising by 0.7%. With fuel prices dropping through
much of the September quarter, we expect overall spending
levels will lift by 1.4%. Importantly, with lower fuel prices
putting money back into households’ pockets, that’s likely to
be underpinned by firm gains in core spending categories.
AUS: Q3 Private CAPEX (%qtr)
Nov 26, Last: –3.6%, WBC f/c: 10.0%
Mkt f/c: 4.5%, Range: 2.0% to 10.0%
Private capex declined 3.6%qtr in Q2 2026 as data centre-

related machinery and equipment investment pulled back
sharply. With nominal ADP equipment imports surging
83%qtr to another record high, we expect a sizeable rebound
in the Q3 figures. Buildings and structures should also find
support as the broader strength in private non-residential
approvals through the first half of 2026 works through the
pipeline. Overall, we expect private capex to grow a strong
10.0%qtr in Q3, with data centres again leading the charge.

Est 3 for 2026/27 capex plans, after our adjustment, is
consistent with impressive nominal growth of 17.9%yr and
real growth of 15.3%yr. We expect Est 4 unadjusted plans for
2026/27 to come in at $221bn, a 15.5% increase on a year ago,
indicative of the scale of investment yet to occur.

Document 295: CEE & CCA week ahead_ Rate decisions in Poland, Kazakhstan to accompany GDP and inflation data

Source PDF: all report pdf/CEE & CCA week ahead_ Rate decisions in Poland, Kazakhstan to accompany GDP and inflation data.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

The National Bank of Poland is expected to keep rates
unchanged, while Kazakhstan faces a close rate decision. GDP
and inﬂation data releases will also make for a busy week
across the region

Adam Antoniak, Peter Virovacz, Muhammet Mercan and Dmitry
Dolgin
We're expecting the National Bank of Poland to keep rates on hold on 2
December
Poland: NBP expected to keep rates
unchanged
On Monday, ﬂash November CPI is likely to show that consumer
inﬂation moderated slightly in Poland, as oil prices and,
consequently, retail petrol prices have been falling in recent weeks.
We estimate that food prices increased after ﬁve consecutive
months of year-on-year declines, while core inﬂation rose back
above 3%, mainly due to a low reference base from November
2025, when core prices declined by 0.1% month-on-month.

### Page 2

Also on Monday, the 3Q26 GDP composition should show
somewhat slower consumption growth amid moderating real wage
growth and pressure from high petrol prices. Meanwhile, ﬁxed
investment likely remained robust, as projects ﬁnanced under the
Domestic Recovery and Resilience Programme were in full swing.
The contribution from net exports was probably weaker than the
0.5ppt reported in 2Q26.
On Wednesday, the National Bank of Poland should keep interest

rates on hold, with the main policy rate remaining at 3.75%. Polish
monetary authorities usually refrain from changing interest rates in
December unless there is an urgent need for a policy adjustment.
Core inﬂation was 0.0% MoM in each of the last two months,
suggesting no indirect impact from higher fuel prices on broader
inﬂation. The government is preparing measures to freeze
household gas and electricity bills in 2027, while oil prices have
moderated. CPI inﬂation is therefore likely to be lower than

presented in the central bank’s November projection.
Hungary: Third-quarter growth likely to
have been sluggish
Hungary's detailed GDP breakdown is due on Tuesday and should
conﬁrm sluggish third-quarter growth. Net exports could surprise
on the positive side, while investment activity was probably the
main drag and consumption the key growth engine. On the
production side, a sharp fall in agriculture and weakness in
construction should be oﬀset by industry and services. A minor

upward revision to the ﬂash estimate is also on the cards.
Thursday brings the November budget data. So far, so good for this
year’s budget execution. Another manageable monthly deﬁcit in
November should be followed by a signiﬁcant improvement in
December as EU funds ﬂow in. The revised full-year target therefore
looks achievable, with the Maastricht deﬁcit potentially even
outperforming the 7.5% of GDP plan.
Turkey: Third-quarter growth expected at

2.9%
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.

Targeting Cookies

### Page 3

Third-quarter GDP data is due on Monday. We expect year-on-year
growth of 2.9%, on the back of resilient services and a relative
recovery in the construction sector despite sluggish performance in
the industry. While early indicators implied a further softening in
4Q26 domestic demand, we currently see full-year GDP growth at
2.8%, with risks tilted to the downside.
November CPI follows on Thursday, when we expect inﬂation to
come in at 0.8% MoM and 29.7% YoY, versus 29.8% a month ago,

supported by the recent decline in energy prices. Risks are on the
upside given pressure on food prices.
CCA: Armenia inﬂation and Kazakhstan's
rate decision
Armenia's November CPI is due on Thursday. We expect November
inﬂation to accelerate noticeably from 4.3% to 4.7% YoY, partly due
to adverse base eﬀects, and partly reﬂecting a genuine increase in
price pressures. Such a print would strengthen our expectation that
the Central Bank of Armenia will deliver another rate hike in

December, following its recent shift towards tighter policy. The
composition will also be important: food inﬂation moderated in
October, while housing and services pressures strengthened despite
the continued support from a strong dram and a somewhat more
restrained ﬁscal policy. A further broadening of domestic price
pressures would provide a strong argument for another rate
increase.
Kazakhstan's base rate decision comes on Friday. We expect the

National Bank of Kazakhstan to cut the base rate by 25bp to
15.50%, although we see this as a relatively low-conviction call,
with a hold the main alternative. Further easing would be
supported by the broader disinﬂation trend, some moderation in
inﬂation expectations and still-high real rates. At the same time,
sticky services inﬂation, a less supportive tenge and persistent
ﬁscal and tariﬀ-related risks argue for caution, while the NBK
previously signalled a low probability of another rate move before

year-end.

The November CPI release on Wednesday, shortly before the
meeting, will therefore be an important input. We expect headline
inﬂation to edge up from 9.2% to 9.4% YoY, mainly due to base

eﬀects, which in itself should not prevent a cut. A stronger
acceleration, particularly if reﬂecting higher tariﬀs and weaker
disinﬂationary support from the tenge, would strengthen the case
for keeping rates unchanged.

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We did not materially alter our real PCE forecast, and expect consumer spending growth to
continue.
Consumer spending has held up remarkably well this year despite higher gasoline costs weighing on
household purchasing power. While we do not expect the recent above-trend near-4% annualized
pace of real consumption growth to be sustained, we see consumers continuing to spend at a healthy

clip into next year. A firmer income backdrop revealed by recent revisions, continued support from
household wealth and an expected recovery in purchasing power as inflation slows should keep
consumption on solid footing. We expect real consumption growth to remain north of 2% in the final
quarter of the year and roughly match this year’s average pace in 2027.
Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products, and Inventories
• A stronger-than-expected capex outturn in Q3 has revealed a slightly firmer trend headed into

next year.
Business investment continues to be driven by the high-tech buildout, with real equipment spending
stronger than expected in Q3 and the latest data suggest a firmer trend. We have not materially
changed our outlook following the Democratic sweep in the midterms. Our forecast already assumes
some moderation in high-tech investment next year and a smaller contribution to GDP growth as the
buildout matures. Beyond high-tech, we expect only a modest broadening in capex spending over the

coming year.
Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Source: U.S. Department of Commerce and Wells Fargo Economics
Investment: Residential
• We have not made major changes to our outlook for residential investment. High mortgage rates
and challenging affordability conditions look set to continue pressuring home sales and new single-
family construction.
Not much has changed in the way of residential investment. Mortgage rates have come in somewhat,

but are still elevated above 7.0%. Building permits are stuck in a low gear, with single-family projects
continuing to moderate against a backdrop of weak buyer demand and elevated inventories.
Multifamily permits have picked up modestly, reflecting renewed strength in apartment demand.
Modestly lower mortgage rates and stabilizing apartment market conditions should help put a floor
beneath residential investment and bring about modest growth over the course of next year.

Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• Structures investment is likely to maintain healthy momentum amid robust data center, power, and
related nonresidential construction.
4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: November 2026 Economics
Structures investment accelerated notably in Q3. The bulk of this momentum can be traced to data
centers and power, however, activity also appears to be broadening out. As examples, private spending
on communication and transportation infrastructure have perked up and manufacturing construction
appears to be stabilizing on increased semiconductor investment. The downturn in commercial
construction has also softened as data centers shore up demand for warehousing. Although new

regulations may soften the pace of data center starts, there are more than enough projects already in
the pipeline to support healthy structures investment through next year.

Net Exports & Trade
• Net exports should remain a fairly persistent drag on U.S. real GDP growth amid continued
imports.
A surge in imports, particularly of high-tech goods like computers, caused net exports to subtract

more than 3 percentage points from Q3 GDP growth. We expect trade to remain a drag in Q4, though
with little data in hand, that largely reflects where trade flows ended last quarter and an assumption
of another sizable gain in imports. Looking ahead, continued strength in high-tech investment should
keep import growth elevated and the trade deficit wide, leaving net exports a persistent drag on U.S.
growth. Exports remain more volatile, but imports are increasingly the bigger driver of the trade story.

Labor Market
• Labor demand has improved modestly in recent months. We now expect payroll growth to average
65K per month in 2027, and the unemployment rate to hold steady at 4.1% over the next four
quarters.
The October jobs report reinforced our view that the labor market remains solid. The pickup in job
growth this year aligns with purchasing manager surveys that point to expanding employment across
the goods and services sectors. At the same time, the historically low trends in initial jobless claims and

the layoff rate suggest employers broadly remain reluctant to shed workers. Structurally, firms appear
focused on retaining existing staff and investing heavily in AI adoption while remaining selective about
adding to head count—a dynamic that should keep the low-hire, low-fire environment largely intact.
We expect payroll growth to average around 65K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate
of breakeven job growth. That should keep the unemployment rate steady at 4.1% through next year

and labor market conditions generally consistent with full employment.
Inflation
• We look for the core PCE deflator to rise 3.3% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to
2.6% in 2027.
The October inflation report reinforced the persistence of price pressures, with upside surprises
emerging across a range of categories. Although gasoline and diesel prices have receded in recent
Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
weeks, earlier increases in transportation costs are still working their way through supply chains. Food
inflation picked up in October and looks poised to strengthen further. The outlook for energy prices
remains highly uncertain and represents the largest swing factor for inflation, in our view. Within the
core, we continue to expect goods inflation to moderate in 2027 as tariff-related pressures fade
further and demand tied to the AI buildout cools. Services inflation, however, is likely to prove stickier.

Health care services prices have been running hot and should continue to face upward pressure from
the aging population, and primary shelter inflation looks to be stabilizing. Taken together, we expect
inflation to remain above the Fed's 2% target through next year and look for the core PCE deflator to
end 2027 at 2.6% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis.

Fiscal Policy
• The lame duck session of Congress probably will not yield a budget reconciliation bill or a debt

ceiling resolution. The December 11 government funding deadline probably will be punted to Q1/

• We project a federal budget deficit of $2.0 trillion for FY 2027, similar to FY 2026 in dollar terms
but a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
Assuming that Congress does not increase or suspend the debt ceiling in the next six weeks, the
incoming Democratic Congress will need to negotiate a resolution with President Trump in 2027.

Right now, it looks like Treasury will hit the debt ceiling around early March. At that point, Treasury can
lean on extraordinary measures and its cash on hand to last for about 7-8 months, putting the debt
ceiling "X date" somewhere around early Q4. We suspect the debt ceiling/annual budget negotiations
will include a lift to defense and nondefense discretionary government spending for FY 2027/2028,
but we doubt the bump will be enough to meaningfully move our economic growth or deficit forecasts.

For now, our working assumption is that divided government will not produce any major new fiscal
expansions/contractions over the next two years, leaving the budget deficit picture as status quo until
after the 2028 election.
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our baseline forecast is for another 25 bp rate hike to the fed funds rate in December, followed by
an extended hold from the FOMC through 2027. The risks are tilted toward 1-2 more hikes early

next year.
• Our forecast for the 10-year Treasury yield at year-end 2027 is 4.85%.
The recent inflation data seem to make a hike in December a lock so long as financial conditions don't
completely implode in the coming weeks. The core CPI came in about 8 bps above consensus at 0.3%
for October, and a 0.4% on core PCE is plausible, depending on how the PPI data shake out. This should
put the FOMC's hawks back in the driver's seat after the October skip.

International Developments
• Global growth has proven more resilient, with our 2026 growth forecast revised higher and a
modest improvement in the underlying 2027 outlook.
• Our 2026 inflation forecast has moved slightly lower, while our 2027 forecast has moved higher,
with inflationary risks likely to persist into 2027.
• The monetary policy bias remains toward extended tightening across advanced economies.

Emerging market policy paths remain differentiated, but have also shifted in a more hawkish
direction, with some central banks bringing forward rate hikes and others having less room to ease
in 2027.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside. Elevated geopolitical uncertainty,
resilient activity and persistent energy and supply-chain pressures increase the risk that inflation
remains higher for longer. Although the direct effects of supply shocks may prove temporary, central

banks are increasingly focused on delayed pass-through, persistent services inflation and potential
second-round effects on wages, prices and inflation expectations. Higher government bond yields are
meanwhile keeping financial conditions tight. The result is a shift toward more extended tightening,
with several rate hikes moving further into the forecast horizon and less room for easing in 2027.
Across advanced economies, the tightening cycle is increasingly extending into 2027. Our European

Central Bank outlook is unchanged, with a 25 bps hike in December still our base case, although
tighter financial conditions raise the risk of a later move. In the U.K., our November Bank of England
hike was realized, and we now expect another 25 bps hike in Q1-2027, reflecting renewed inflation
pressures associated with the Ofgem price-cap reset.
Elsewhere, the policy outlook has turned more hawkish in Canada. Having previously flagged upside
risks to our year-end call, we now expect the Bank of Canada to hike at consecutive meetings, bringing

the policy rate to 3.00% by Q1-2027. In Australia, the Reserve Bank of Australia raised the Cash Rate
again in November. After five hikes, we think policymakers have room to assess how tighter policy
feeds through the economy, although risks remain skewed toward another increase if inflation stays
elevated or activity proves stronger than expected. Japan is unchanged: we continue to expect the
Bank of Japan to hike in December and again in 2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.

resilient growth and rising inflation have led us to bring forward our second Reserve Bank of India

### Page 1

A New Keynesian ‘plucking’ model of unemployment and inflation
PRELIMINARY AND INCOMPLETE
Anthony Brassil* and Callum Ryan*
Research Discussion Paper
20XX-YY
December 2025
*Economic Research Department
Reserve Bank of Australia
The views expressed in this paper are those of the authors and should not be attributed to the
Reserve Bank of Australia. Any errors are the sole responsibility of the authors.
Author: ryanca at domain rba.gov.au
External Communications: rbainfo@rba.gov.au

https://doi.org/10.47688/rdp20XX-YY

### Page 2

Abstract
We construct a New Keynesian model with search-and-matching frictions in the labour market that
can generate the nonlinear ‘plucking’ dynamics of unemployment in the data: unemployment tends to
spike upwards and then slowly decline. The model features endogenous layoffs, a ‘slippery’ job ladder,
downward nominal wage rigidity, and convex recruiting costs. It generates both the ‘deepness’ and
‘steepness’ asymmetries in unemployment dynamics, and broadly matches the response of labour-

market flows in a downturn. The model endogeneously generates a Phillips curve relationship that
is strongly nonlinear and state-dependent, and a short-run non-accelerating rate of unemployment
(NAIRU) that fluctuates asymmetrically over the business cycle. We also show that cyclical properties
(e.g. shock volatility, nominal rigidities, policy regime) can have a significant effect on the long-run
NAIRU via agents’ precautionary response to future uncertainty.

Keywords:
